## 3. Endpoint wiring summary

Every (route, endpoint) pair found statically. Statuses:
- `OK`: a backend controller (or BFF handler) serves this path and method.
- `WRONG_METHOD`: the path exists but not with this method.
- `NO_ROUTE`: no backend controller serves this path (after stripping `/api/v1`).
- `PROXY_BLOCKED`: web call through `/api/patient/*` that `patient-web/lib/api/patient-allowlist.ts` rejects (the proxy answers 404), checked with a sample UUID for each `:param`.
- `PROXY_BLOCKED?`: as above, but the path has params; it is blocked for a UUID, a slug and every short string literal of the calling file. Confirm with the values the screen really sends.
- `NO_HANDLER`: web call to `/api/*` with no route handler in `patient-web/app/api`.
- `EXTERNAL`: AI service (`EXPO_PUBLIC_AI_URL`), not checked here.
- `PARTIAL`: the path ends in a variable suffix (`${id}${suffix}`); only the prefix is known (shown with `/…`), so it is not checked.

| App | Pairs | OK | WRONG_METHOD | NO_ROUTE | PROXY_BLOCKED | PROXY_BLOCKED? | NO_HANDLER | EXTERNAL | PARTIAL | unresolved calls (built from variables) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| patient-app | 443 | 441 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 2 | 5 |
| patient-web | 1120 | 1111 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 7 | 2 |

Totals: 1563 pairs, 1552 OK, 9 partial (not checkable), 2 need attention.

## 3b. Calls built from variables: how each was resolved

Unresolved calls now: **patient-app 5, patient-web 2**. The tool used to count the same few call sites once for every route that imports them. Every site is now one of the kinds below (`DEBUG_UNRESOLVED=1 node tools/design/screen-inventory.mjs` lists any that remain).

### Generic wrappers: the path is attributed at each caller

A wrapper forwards its own parameter to `fetch` (`fetch(patientApiUrl(path))`), so the call cannot name a path where it is written; each caller of the wrapper is scanned and carries its own path. The table counts those callers, so a caller that hides its path would show up in the last column.

| Wrapper | Forwards at | Callers | Path resolved at caller | Forwards again | Not resolvable |

| `useSection` | `patient-app/src/components/consult/AppointmentSections.tsx:72` | 3 | 3 | 0 | 0 |
| `(anonymous)` | `patient-app/src/hooks/useSearchSuggest.ts:12` | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| `apiFetch` | `patient-app/src/utils/api.ts:187` | 396 | 385 | 9 | `patient-app/src/components/orders/OrderList.tsx:82`, `patient-app/src/utils/useCatalogNames.ts:32` |

| `fetchPublicList` | `patient-app/src/utils/publicCache.ts:50` | 12 | 10 | 2 | 0 |
| `probeStoredFile` | `patient-app/src/utils/scanProbe.ts:17` | 1 | 0 | 0 | `patient-app/src/components/health/ProfileView.tsx:58` |

| `optionalJson` | `patient-web/app/[locale]/dashboard/page.tsx:68` | 5 | 5 | 0 | 0 |

## 5b. Web screens with no API calls

Each of the 7 was classified by reading its page and the components it imports (verdicts live in `inventory/static-screens.json`). SUSPICIOUS and UNTRACED verdicts are always listed in section 7 (Needs review); a pointer page whose copy claims more than it shows can be there too.

| Route | File | Verdict | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/delivery/address-select` | `patient-web/app/[locale]/delivery/address-select/page.tsx` | ?redirect | Server redirect to /profile/addresses?select=1 (the address book in pick mode), which calls the API. |
| `/diagnostics/insurance-upload` | `patient-web/app/[locale]/diagnostics/insurance-upload/page.tsx` | ?redirect | Server redirect to /diagnostics/insurance-approval, keeping the order id; that screen calls the API. |
| `/drug-scanner` | `patient-web/app/[locale]/drug-scanner/page.tsx` | ?redirect | Server redirect to /pharmacy/barcode (the scanner screen), which calls the API. |
| `/login/app-callback` | `patient-web/app/[locale]/login/app-callback/page.tsx` | STATIC (by design) | Opens the native app with the sign-in result (window.location.replace to the app link built by lib/auth/app-callback); no API call of its own. |
| `/login/social-callback` | `patient-web/app/[locale]/login/social-callback/page.tsx` | STATIC (by design) | Popup page: posts the OAuth code and state to the opener window (postMessage); the opener (login page) calls the API. |
| `/offline` | `patient-web/app/[locale]/offline/page.tsx` | STATIC (by design) | Offline fallback state page (RouteState text and a retry link); nothing to fetch by design. |
| `/voice` | `patient-web/app/[locale]/voice/page.tsx` | STATIC (by design) | Launcher: seven links defined in the page plus components-next/voice-command-button.tsx, which uses the browser SpeechRecognition API and navigates by keyword; no API call. |

## 6. Mock / placeholder found (heuristic)

**Heuristic scan, not a proof.** `tools/design/mock-scan.mjs` reads the TypeScript AST of 1437 source files (the files each route reaches, the route trees and their layouts, and the Batch 0 component folders; 65 of them are Batch 0) and lists code that looks like fake data or a dead control. A hit is a lead to check, a miss is not a guarantee. Each pattern was sampled on real hits and tightened until false positives were rare; when a pattern finds nothing it says so.

Ignored: tests and fixtures, `node_modules`, the generated mirror `patient-web/components-next/ui-generated`, i18n dictionaries, `placeholder=` attributes and props (input hints), `console.*` arguments and error messages. **B0** = Batch 0: its route files, the files beside a web Batch 0 route, `patient-web/components-next/{auth,home,core}` and `patient-app/src/components/{auth,home,navigation,screen}`.

| Category | app B0 | app rest | web B0 | web rest | Total |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dead buttons and links | 0 | 1 | 0 | 4 | 5 |
| Simulated success (a timer confirms, no API call) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Math.random (value-like) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Mock / sample / dummy / fake / demo constants | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Hard-coded data arrays (price / rating / distance / fee fields) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 3 | 3 |
| Hard-coded ratings, review counts, prices, distances | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 |
| Dummy-looking values (names, phones, emails, lorem) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| TODO / dummy / coming-soon text shown to users | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| TODO / FIXME / stub comments (developer-facing) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| **All** | 0 | 1 | 0 | 8 | 9 |

## 7. Needs review

1064 open items, merged from `docs/design/needs-review/*.json` (format in that folder's README). These are things an agent found while rebuilding or auditing a screen and could not settle from the client code. **The reviewer session verifies each one and fixes backend gaps; a design session only reports.** Per-screen element audits are in [`docs/design/audit/`](audit/).


### Batch 0 — patient-app

| Screen | Element | file:line | What was found | What is suspected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/` | GET /home/upcoming-appointment with no appointment | `backend/src/modules/home/home.controller.ts` | Runtime check against the seeded backend: the endpoint answers 200 with an empty body (not JSON, not null) when the patient has no upcoming appointment. The web dashboard copes; whether the app's apiFetch handles an empty 200 body without throwing was not run (the app was not executed). | A 200 with no body breaks clients that call res.json(). Suggest `null` or 204, and confirm apiFetch tolerates it. |
| `/` | Next appointment card: doctor name | `backend/src/modules/home/home.service.ts:74` | getUpcomingAppointment reads the doctor name from provider_profiles with doctorName = prov?.name \|\| ''. The provider profile schema declares no top-level name (name_ar, name_en, display_name_ar, display_name_en: backend/src/schemas/provider-profile.schema.ts:25-26,215-216; the only 'name' is inside the facility roster sub-schema, line 144). The result is null, and Home hides the doctor line when it is null. The same field is read in getOffers (line 41) and the package search (lines 156) for provider names. | Unsure whether older documents hold a name field; by the schema the card never shows the doctor. Read display_name_ar / name_ar (backend). |
| `/` | Route collisions: '/' (three files) and '/services' (two files) | `patient-app/app/index.tsx:16` | Three files resolve to the URL '/': app/index.tsx (splash), app/(tabs)/index.tsx (Home) and app/(onboarding)/index.tsx (intro). Two resolve to '/services': app/(tabs)/services.tsx (Services tab: tiles and search pill) and app/services/index.tsx ('All services' guide, 23 rows). Callers using the bare URL: Home 'كل الخدمات' router.push('/services') (app/(tabs)/index.tsx:138) and the notifications back fallback router.replace('/') (app/notifications/index.tsx:160). Callers that include the group name ('/(tabs)', '/(onboarding)/language') are not ambiguous. The screen inventory lists both pairs under the same route. | OWNER DECISION or a device check. Fixed here: the notifications back fallback now uses '/(tabs)' (no longer the bare '/'). Left: two files still resolve to '/services' (app/(tabs)/services.tsx, the tab screen, and app/services/index.tsx, the 23-row guide), and Home 'كل الخدمات' pushes the bare '/services'. Which one is the destination of that row? The answer (the guide, or the tab screen) decides which file is renamed or given its own path; I cannot determine expo-router's tie-break without a device. '/' is also shared by app/index.tsx (splash) and app/(onboarding)/index.tsx; that follows the onboarding decision above. Approved 2026-10-10 (owner, #409), '/services' part fixed on design/owner-small: 'All services' opens the Services tab (Home pushes '/(tabs)/services'), the 23-row guide app/services/index.tsx and its data are deleted, so '/services' now has one file and old deep links open the tab. |
| `/(onboarding)` | Onboarding intro and language screens: no entry point (permissions screen removed) | `patient-app/app/(onboarding)/intro.tsx:67` | No file in app/ or src/ outside app/(onboarding) pushes or replaces to /(onboarding) (grep for '(onboarding)'); the only references are the Stack.Screen in app/_layout.tsx:98 and a type in src/types/index.ts:651. The three screens chain to each other (intro to language to permissions to /(auth)/welcome) but nothing starts the chain. | OWNER DECISION: the launch rule (first launch Welcome, later launches Home as a guest) is now in the splash and Welcome already carries language and theme. Should the three finished onboarding screens (intro slides, language, permissions) come before Welcome on the first launch, or be removed? If they stay, the first launch becomes intro -> language -> permissions -> Welcome and the splash sends a first launch to /(onboarding) instead of Welcome; if they go, delete app/(onboarding) and the permissions request needs another home (Home, at the first use of the camera or location). Until decided they stay unreachable except by deep link. APPROVED 2026-10-10 (owner decision 6) and fixed on design/onboarding-first-launch: the first launch is now Language -> Intro -> Welcome once (splash launchRoute, flag INTRO_DONE), the permissions screen is deleted and each permission is asked in context (camera, photos, location already did; notifications now from NotificationAsk on the offers screen and the notification settings; no prompt at app start). The intro file moved from index.tsx to intro.tsx so it no longer shares the URL / with the splash. |
| `/consultations/doctor-profile` | Legacy screen | `patient-app/app/consultations/doctor-profile.tsx:1` | [client] The file still has @ts-nocheck and draws with the legacy useApp colors and AppText; it only redirects, so left as is. | Client-side: later batch. |

Lighthouse lists render-blocking CSS (three stylesheets, about 28 KB gz) and the theme/nonce script as the savings. | F82 is a rendering-path problem (render-blocking CSS, font loading, server time to first byte on /pharmacy), not a bundle-size one. Needs its own change; not started. |
| `all routes` | Shared JS still over the 170 KB gz budget after the #286 trim | `patient-web/next.config.ts` | Measured with Lighthouse (median of 3, production build, seeded backend), script transfer on /ar/pharmacy and /ar/consultations/doctors went from 224 to about 177 KB and on /ar from 224 to about 196 KB. What is left is the React and Next client runtime (about 113 KB gz in two chunks), the next-intl ICU message parser (about 12 KB gz: @formatjs/icu-messageformat-parser, intl-messageformat, use-intl), and components-next/web-mcp-provider.tsx (3.4 KB gz: six languages of tool descriptions shipped to every page, registering tools on a navigator.modelContext that nothing reads unless a WebMCP-capable browser provides it). | The budget cannot be met by design-system changes alone. Candidates for a separate change: load web-mcp-provider only when navigator.modelContext exists (or send only the current locale's descriptions from the server), and precompile the ICU messages so the parser leaves the client. Both are outside the design track, so reported here. |

### Batch 0 — repo

| Screen | Element | file:line | What was found | What is suspected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tooling` | tools/design/import-rule.baseline.json is not on main | `tools/design/import-rule.baseline.json:226` | The import rule (P12.C2) and its baseline exist on fix/audit-2026-09 only; main has neither. patient-app AuthKit no longer imports @expo/vector-icons (BrandMark in packages/ui-native), and running the rule from fix/audit against this branch reports AuthKit as clean and `--update` removes its baseline line (226) and its `exceptions` entry (230). | When fix/audit-2026-09 reaches main, run `node tools/design/import-rule.mjs --update`; it lowers the baseline from 224 to 223 files and drops the exception. I did not copy the rule onto this branch, to avoid an add/add conflict with the reviewer's copy. |
